occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When I'm alone in the car or in a quiet client's house, I hear a voice clear as somebody standing beside me saying I failed them and should be punished, but when I look nobody is there.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most days I feel heavy and empty from the minute I open my eyes, like the person who used to go to church, Zumba, and help the kids at the center is just gone.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: Everything I do has gotten slow, even putting on my scrubs or answering a question, and people at work keep telling me I just stand there staring like I can't make myself move.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep going over every little thing I ever did wrong with patients or my family, ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I think about dying a lot, especially driving home in the morning, wishing I could just not wake up or disappear so everybody would be safer without me.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur exclusively during the major depressive episode\n  manifestation: I never had voices or those beliefs about hurting clients back when I was feeling like myself; they only started after my mood sank and everything began feeling hopeless.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I can't keep up with my care visits safely anymore, and my supervisor had to stop me during a care-plan visit because I was too overwhelmed and stuck to finish It’s like I’ll get off work and I should be able to sleep because I’m so tired, but I just lie there awake. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared and it doesn’t feel like I rested at all. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up scared like that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I do finally fall asleep after work, I’ll wake up scared, like my body never really settled down. And then I just feel like I didn’t actually rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these sleep problems and waking up scared before you started working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure. I don’t think it was like this before, not this broken up. The overnight shifts seem to be when I really noticed it—getting off work exhausted but still just lying there wide awake, and then if I do fall asleep, waking up scared like I didn’t rest at all. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. [
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F51.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess it’s mostly my sleep. I don’t want to make it sound too dramatic, but it’s gotten pretty messed up. I work overnight shifts, and even when I get off and I’m exhausted, I’ll just lie there wide awake. Then if I do fall asleep, I wake up scared, and it feels like I didn’t really rest at all. I thought maybe I should come in and ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after you began working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe the overnight shifts made it worse, yeah. It’s like I’ll get off work and I should be able to sleep because I’m so tired, but I just lie there awake. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared and it doesn’t feel like I rested at all. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up scared like that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I do finally fall asleep after work, I’ll wake up scared, like my body never really settled down. And then I just feel like I didn’t actually rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these sleep problems and waking up scared before you started working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure. I don’t think it was like this before, not this broken up. The overnight shifts seem to be when I really noticed it—getting off work exhausted but still just lying there wide awake, and then if I do fall asleep, waking up scared like I didn’t rest at all. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any significant stress, anxiety, or other emotional difficulties around the same time your sleep problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I was feeling pretty low around then. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and I just feel heavy and empty right away. Like… the person I used to be, going to church, doing Zumba, helping with the kids at the center… it feels like she’s just kind of gone. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities like Zumba and helping with the kids at the center, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that right. I mean, I used to like those things, yeah. Lately I haven’t really been doing them much, but I don’t know if that means I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just… tired and not myself. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care, because I do care. It’s just changed, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you are no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m no longer able to enjoy them. That sounds so final, you know? I just haven’t really felt like myself with them lately. Sometimes I think maybe if I went, I might feel better, but then I don’t go. So… maybe it’s less enjoyment, or maybe I’m just not getting there. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s kind of there from when I open my eyes. I feel heavy and empty, like… that version of me who went to church and Zumba and helped with the kids just isn’t there right now. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “at all.” I don’t want to answer it wrong. It’s more like… things don’t really pull me in the way they used to, and I have a hard time getting myself to do them. Maybe if I was there I could feel something, I don’t know. I just haven’t been feeling much pleasure from them lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. Maybe little things sometimes, like if something is on TV or someone says something funny, I might smile for a second. But I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly. Nothing really stands out the way it used to. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares every time, but I do wake up scared sometimes. My sleep’s just really broken up now… I get off an overnight shift and I still lie there wide awake, and then when I finally drift off, I wake up feeling like I didn’t rest at all. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your waking up scared is related to any specific dreams or thoughts you're having?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t always remember a specific dream, so I don’t want to say it’s because of that if it isn’t. It’s more like… my sleep is just really broken up. I get off the overnight shift and I’m still lying there wide awake, and then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared sometimes and feel like I didn’t really sleep at all. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

I thought maybe I should come in and ask about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your sleep problems started after you began working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe the overnight shifts made it worse, yeah. It’s like I’ll get off work and I should be able to sleep because I’m so tired, but I just lie there awake. And then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared and it doesn’t feel like I rested at all. I don’t know if that answers it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up scared like that?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty often, I guess. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but when I do finally fall asleep after work, I’ll wake up scared, like my body never really settled down. And then I just feel like I didn’t actually rest at all.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience these sleep problems and waking up scared before you started working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not totally sure. I don’t think it was like this before, not this broken up. The overnight shifts seem to be when I really noticed it—getting off work exhausted but still just lying there wide awake, and then if I do fall asleep, waking up scared like I didn’t rest at all. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Did you experience any significant stress, anxiety, or other emotional difficulties around the same time your sleep problems started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I guess I was feeling pretty low around then. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up and I just feel heavy and empty right away. Like… the person I used to be, going to church, doing Zumba, helping with the kids at the center… it feels like she’s just kind of gone. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy activities like Zumba and helping with the kids at the center, or has that changed for you?\n\nPatient: I’m… not really sure how to answer that right. I mean, I used to like those things, yeah. Lately I haven’t really been doing them much, but I don’t know if that means I don’t enjoy them or if I’m just… tired and not myself. I don’t want to make it sound like I don’t care, because I do care. It’s just changed, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you are no longer able to enjoy things that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know if I’d say I’m no longer able to enjoy them. That sounds so final, you know? I just haven’t really felt like myself with them lately. Sometimes I think maybe if I went, I might feel better, but then I don’t go. So… maybe it’s less enjoyment, or maybe I’m just not getting there. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad or empty most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most days, I think so. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it’s kind of there from when I open my eyes. I feel heavy and empty, like… that version of me who went to church and Zumba and helped with the kids just isn’t there right now. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there things that you used to enjoy doing, but now you don't find pleasure in them at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d say “at all.” I don’t want to answer it wrong. It’s more like… things don’t really pull me in the way they used to, and I have a hard time getting myself to do them. Maybe if I was there I could feel something, I don’t know. I just haven’t been feeling much pleasure from them lately.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: I… I’m trying to think. Maybe little things sometimes, like if something is on TV or someone says something funny, I might smile for a second. But I don’t know if I’d call it pleasure exactly. Nothing really stands out the way it used to. Is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yeah. I don’t know if I’d call them nightmares every time, but I do wake up scared sometimes. My sleep’s just really broken up now… I get off an overnight shift and I still lie there wide awake, and then when I finally drift off, I wake up feeling like I didn’t rest at all. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your waking up scared is related to any specific dreams or thoughts you're having?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I don’t always remember a specific dream, so I don’t want to say it’s because of that if it isn’t. It’s more like… my sleep is just really broken up. I get off the overnight shift and I’m still lying there wide awake, and then when I finally do drift off, I wake up scared sometimes and feel like I didn’t really sleep at all. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have a normal, uninterrupted night's sleep when you're not working an overnight shift?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but even when I’m not on the overnight shift, my sleep still feels broken up. I’ll lie there awake, and if I do finally drift off, I can wake up scared and then it feels like I didn’t rest at all. Is that the kind of answer you mean?\n\nDoctor: Has something very stressful or traumatic happened to you around the time your sleep problems started?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it traumatic, exactly… I mean, people go through worse. But it did start around when my agency changed me to rotating overnight shifts, and they added extra paperwork too. That’s been going on for about a year and two months now. I guess that’s when my sleep really started getting messed up.\n\nDoctor: Did something very stressful or traumatic happen to you before you started working overnight shifts?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not that I can point to. Nothing big happened right before that, at least nothing I’d call traumatic. It was more when the overnight shifts started that things seemed to change. Is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
